<analysis>**original_problem_statement:**
The user is building a comprehensive ERP system for their dairy farm. The initial scope included bug fixes and core HR features. The project has since evolved to include a hierarchical Warehouse Management System, a material consumption workflow, a driver scheduling system, data import capabilities, and complex permission management.

**PRODUCT REQUIREMENTS (based on latest user requests):**
1.  **Feature (P0):** Create a new UI page/component to manage and approve pending requests for extra pay (for work on weekends/holidays).
2.  **Feature (P0):** In the Official Letter Request form, when the user selects Leave Request as the letter type, a date range picker should dynamically appear to allow them to specify the leave dates.
3.  **Refactor (P0):** Reorganize the main sidebar navigation:
    *   Merge the Advanced Reports page/link into the main Reports section.
    *   Move Appearance Settings, Notification Settings, and Camera System into the System Settings section.
    *   Move Payroll, Salary Structure, and Employee Scheduling into the Human Resources section.
4.  **Bug Fix (P1):** Investigate and fix a non-functional update button in the attendance view, which was reported in a previous session but never fully resolved.

**User's preferred language**: العربية (Arabic)

**what currently exists?**
-   The  page, which was previously broken, has been fixed. The root cause was a duplicated  prefix in the URL. The page has now been integrated as a tab within the Warehouse Management page.
-   A new Driver Schedule feature has been implemented, including a backend model, API routes, and a frontend page. This feature has been integrated as a tab within the Employee Scheduling page.
-   The permissions system has been completely overhauled. All permissions were wiped from all users, and full administrative rights were granted *only* to the IT Manager (Yasir Ahmed Hassan Kashob). The backend logic that automatically granted permissions based on job titles (e.g., General Manager) has been removed to enforce a manual grant-only system.
-   A new feature to import milk reception data from Excel/CSV files has been added to the Milk Reception page. This includes a backend API using  to process the file and a frontend dialog for uploading. The import template now matches all fields on the manual entry form.
-   The payroll calculation logic has been significantly improved.
    -   A bug causing incorrect absence calculations due to wrong weekend-day data ( instead of ) has been fixed for all employees.
    -   An approval workflow for overtime/extra pay has been implemented. The system no longer automatically adds pay for weekend work; it now requires explicit approval via new backend APIs.

**Last working item**:
-   **Last item agent was working:** Acknowledging and planning a large set of UI refactoring and new feature requests from the user. The agent had just finished implementing the backend for the overtime approval workflow and was about to start building the frontend for it and reorganizing the application's sidebar navigation.

The testing agent should be used to verify that all links have been moved correctly, the old routes are gone, the new pages load, and there are no console errors.

**Code Architecture**


**Key Technical Concepts**
-   **Component Embedding & UI Refactoring:** Used a tabbed interface () to merge related pages ( into ,  into ), making the UI cleaner. This was achieved by passing an  prop to child components to conditionally render their titles.
-   **Data Import from Excel/CSV:** Implemented a backend endpoint using  and  to read uploaded files, map column names (handling both Arabic and English), create new entities (suppliers) if they don't exist, and batch-insert data.
-   **Approval Workflow:** Modified a core business logic process (payroll calculation) to depend on a new approval state. Extra pay is now only calculated if an associated record () has been explicitly approved via new, dedicated APIs.
-   **Centralized, Manual Permissions:** Refactored the permissions API to remove all implicit, role-based permission grants. The system now relies exclusively on explicit, manually granted permissions stored in the database, providing stricter access control.

**key DB schema**
-   **hr_employees:**  (Standardized to  for Friday/Saturday).
-   **driver_schedules:** New collection to store driver-specific daily schedules.
-   **user_permissions:** This collection is now the primary source of truth for manually granted permissions.
-   **attendance:** Existing collection now implicitly used for the extra pay approval workflow (logic checks for an  flag before calculation, though the flag itself is not stored).

**All files of reference**
-   **/app/frontend/src/components/Layout.jsx**: Will need heavy modification for the next task.
-   **/app/frontend/src/pages/EmployeeScheduling.jsx**: Example of the new tabbed layout.
-   **/app/frontend/src/pages/WarehouseManagement.jsx**: Example of the new tabbed layout.
-   **/app/backend/server.py**: Contains the new payroll logic and import functionality.
-   **/app/backend/routes/permissions_routes.py**: Contains the updated, stricter permission logic.

-   ** (CRITICAL):** Still contains a large amount of routing logic that should be moved to dedicated files in .
-   ** (HIGH):** The user has explicitly requested a significant refactoring of the sidebar menu structure.
-   ** /  (MEDIUM):** These files are now very large as they act as containers for multiple tabs. They could potentially be broken down further into smaller components.

**key api endpoints**
-   **POST **: NEW - Uploads and processes an Excel/CSV file of milk reception data.
-   **GET **: NEW - Downloads a template for the import.
-   **GET **: NEW - Lists all attendance records for weekend/holiday work that need approval for extra pay.
-   **POST **: NEW - Approves an extra pay request.
-   **POST **: NEW - Rejects an extra pay request.
-   **GET, POST, PUT, DELETE **: NEW - CRUD endpoints for managing driver schedules.
-   **GET **: MODIFIED - No longer returns permissions based on role/position, only from granted permissions.

self = <PreparedRequest [POST]>, url = '/api/auth/login', params = OrderedDict()

    def prepare_url(self, url, params):
        """Prepares the given HTTP URL."""
        #: Accept objects that have string representations.
        #: We're unable to blindly call unicode/str functions
        #: as this will include the bytestring indicator (b'')
        #: on python 3.x.
        #: https://github.com/psf/requests/pull/2238
        if isinstance(url, bytes):
            url = url.decode("utf8")
        else:
            url = str(url)
    
        # Remove leading whitespaces from url
        url = url.lstrip()
    
        # Don't do any URL preparation for non-HTTP schemes like `mailto`,
        # `data` etc to work around exceptions from `url_parse`, which
        # handles RFC 3986 only.
        if ":" in url and not url.lower().startswith("http"):
            self.url = url
            return
    
        # Support for unicode domain names and paths.
        try:
            scheme, auth, host, port, path, query, fragment = parse_url(url)
        except LocationParseError as e:
            raise InvalidURL(*e.args)
    
        if not scheme:
>           raise MissingSchema(
                f"Invalid URL {url!r}: No scheme supplied. "
                f"Perhaps you meant https://{url}?"
            )
E           requests.exceptions.MissingSchema: Invalid URL '/api/auth/login': No scheme supplied. Perhaps you meant https:///api/auth/login?

self = <PreparedRequest [POST]>, url = '/api/auth/login', params = OrderedDict()

    def prepare_url(self, url, params):
        """Prepares the given HTTP URL."""
        #: Accept objects that have string representations.
        #: We're unable to blindly call unicode/str functions
        #: as this will include the bytestring indicator (b'')
        #: on python 3.x.
        #: https://github.com/psf/requests/pull/2238
        if isinstance(url, bytes):
            url = url.decode("utf8")
        else:
            url = str(url)
    
        # Remove leading whitespaces from url
        url = url.lstrip()
    
        # Don't do any URL preparation for non-HTTP schemes like `mailto`,
        # `data` etc to work around exceptions from `url_parse`, which
        # handles RFC 3986 only.
        if ":" in url and not url.lower().startswith("http"):
            self.url = url
            return
    
        # Support for unicode domain names and paths.
        try:
            scheme, auth, host, port, path, query, fragment = parse_url(url)
        except LocationParseError as e:
            raise InvalidURL(*e.args)
    
        if not scheme:
>           raise MissingSchema(
                f"Invalid URL {url!r}: No scheme supplied. "
                f"Perhaps you meant https://{url}?"
            )
E           requests.exceptions.MissingSchema: Invalid URL '/api/auth/login': No scheme supplied. Perhaps you meant https:///api/auth/login?

    def prepare_url(self, url, params):
        """Prepares the given HTTP URL."""
        #: Accept objects that have string representations.
        #: We're unable to blindly call unicode/str functions
        #: as this will include the bytestring indicator (b'')
        #: on python 3.x.
        #: https://github.com/psf/requests/pull/2238
        if isinstance(url, bytes):
            url = url.decode("utf8")
        else:
            url = str(url)
    
        # Remove leading whitespaces from url
        url = url.lstrip()
    
        # Don't do any URL preparation for non-HTTP schemes like `mailto`,
        # `data` etc to work around exceptions from `url_parse`, which
        # handles RFC 3986 only.
        if ":" in url and not url.lower().startswith("http"):
            self.url = url
            return
    
        # Support for unicode domain names and paths.
        try:
            scheme, auth, host, port, path, query, fragment = parse_url(url)
        except LocationParseError as e:
            raise InvalidURL(*e.args)

self = <PreparedRequest [POST]>, url = '/api/auth/login', params = OrderedDict()

    def prepare_url(self, url, params):
        """Prepares the given HTTP URL."""
        #: Accept objects that have string representations.
        #: We're unable to blindly call unicode/str functions
        #: as this will include the bytestring indicator (b'')
        #: on python 3.x.
        #: https://github.com/psf/requests/pull/2238
        if isinstance(url, bytes):
            url = url.decode("utf8")
        else:
            url = str(url)
    
        # Remove leading whitespaces from url
        url = url.lstrip()
    
        # Don't do any URL preparation for non-HTTP schemes like `mailto`,
        # `data` etc to work around exceptions from `url_parse`, which
        # handles RFC 3986 only.
        if ":" in url and not url.lower().startswith("http"):
            self.url = url
            return
    
        # Support for unicode domain names and paths.
        try:
            scheme, auth, host, port, path, query, fragment = parse_url(url)
        except LocationParseError as e:
            raise InvalidURL(*e.args)
    
        if not scheme:

self = <Response [520]>, kwargs = {}

    def json(self, **kwargs):
        r"""Decodes the JSON response body (if any) as a Python object.
    
        This may return a dictionary, list, etc. depending on what is in the response.
    
        :param \*\*kwargs: Optional arguments that ``json.loads`` takes.
        :raises requests.exceptions.JSONDecodeError: If the response body does not
            contain valid json.
        """
    
        if not self.encoding and self.content and len(self.content) > 3:
            # No encoding set. JSON RFC 4627 section 3 states we should expect
            # UTF-8, -16 or -32. Detect which one to use; If the detection or
            # decoding fails, fall back to `self.text` (using charset_normalizer to make
            # a best guess).
            encoding = guess_json_utf(self.content)
            if encoding is not None:
                try:
                    return complexjson.loads(self.content.decode(encoding), **kwargs)
                except UnicodeDecodeError:
                    # Wrong UTF codec detected; usually because it's not UTF-8
                    # but some other 8-bit codec.  This is an RFC violation,
                    # and the server didn't bother to tell us what codec *was*
                    # used.
                    pass
                except JSONDecodeError as e:
                    raise RequestsJSONDecodeError(e.msg, e.doc, e.pos)
    
        try:
>           return complexjson.loads(self.text, **kwargs)
                   ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

self = <Response [520]>, kwargs = {}

    def json(self, **kwargs):
        r"""Decodes the JSON response body (if any) as a Python object.
    
        This may return a dictionary, list, etc. depending on what is in the response.
    
        :param \*\*kwargs: Optional arguments that ``json.loads`` takes.
        :raises requests.exceptions.JSONDecodeError: If the response body does not
            contain valid json.
        """
    
        if not self.encoding and self.content and len(self.content) > 3:
            # No encoding set. JSON RFC 4627 section 3 states we should expect
            # UTF-8, -16 or -32. Detect which one to use; If the detection or
            # decoding fails, fall back to `self.text` (using charset_normalizer to make
            # a best guess).
            encoding = guess_json_utf(self.content)
            if encoding is not None:
                try:
                    return complexjson.loads(self.content.decode(encoding), **kwargs)
                except UnicodeDecodeError:
                    # Wrong UTF codec detected; usually because it's not UTF-8
                    # but some other 8-bit codec.  This is an RFC violation,
                    # and the server didn't bother to tell us what codec *was*
                    # used.
                    pass
                except JSONDecodeError as e:
                    raise RequestsJSONDecodeError(e.msg, e.doc, e.pos)

    def json(self, **kwargs):
        r"""Decodes the JSON response body (if any) as a Python object.

            # decoding fails, fall back to `self.text` (using charset_normalizer to make
            # a best guess).

                except UnicodeDecodeError:

                    # but some other 8-bit codec.  This is an RFC violation,
                    # and the server didn't bother to tell us what codec *was*
                    # used.

self = <Response [520]>, kwargs = {}

    def json(self, **kwargs):
        r"""Decodes the JSON response body (if any) as a Python object.
    
        This may return a dictionary, list, etc. depending on what is in the response.
    
        :param \*\*kwargs: Optional arguments that ``json.loads`` takes.
        :raises requests.exceptions.JSONDecodeError: If the response body does not
            contain valid json.
        """
    
        if not self.encoding and self.content and len(self.content) > 3:
            # No encoding set. JSON RFC 4627 section 3 states we should expect
            # UTF-8, -16 or -32. Detect which one to use; If the detection or
            # decoding fails, fall back to `self.text` (using charset_normalizer to make
            # a best guess).
            encoding = guess_json_utf(self.content)
            if encoding is not None:
                try:
                    return complexjson.loads(self.content.decode(encoding), **kwargs)
                except UnicodeDecodeError:
                    # Wrong UTF codec detected; usually because it's not UTF-8
                    # but some other 8-bit codec.  This is an RFC violation,
                    # and the server didn't bother to tell us what codec *was*
                    # used.
                    pass
                except JSONDecodeError as e:
                    raise RequestsJSONDecodeError(e.msg, e.doc, e.pos)

            return complexjson.loads(self.text, **kwargs)
        except JSONDecodeError as e:
            # Catch JSON-related errors and raise as requests.JSONDecodeError

    def json(self, **kwargs):
        r"""Decodes the JSON response body (if any) as a Python object.

            # decoding fails, fall back to `self.text` (using charset_normalizer to make
            # a best guess).

                except UnicodeDecodeError:

                    # but some other 8-bit codec.  This is an RFC violation,
                    # and the server didn't bother to tell us what codec *was*

    def json(self, **kwargs):
        r"""Decodes the JSON response body (if any) as a Python object.
    
        This may return a dictionary, list, etc. depending on what is in the response.
    
        :param \*\*kwargs: Optional arguments that ``json.loads`` takes.
        :raises requests.exceptions.JSONDecodeError: If the response body does not
            contain valid json.
        """
    
        if not self.encoding and self.content and len(self.content) > 3:
            # No encoding set. JSON RFC 4627 section 3 states we should expect
            # UTF-8, -16 or -32. Detect which one to use; If the detection or
            # decoding fails, fall back to `self.text` (using charset_normalizer to make
            # a best guess).

                    # but some other 8-bit codec.  This is an RFC violation,
                    # and the server didn't bother to tell us what codec *was*

                    pass

            # decoding fails, fall back to `self.text` (using charset_normalizer to make

    def json(self, **kwargs):
        r"""Decodes the JSON response body (if any) as a Python object.
    
        This may return a dictionary, list, etc. depending on what is in the response.
    
        :param \*\*kwargs: Optional arguments that ``json.loads`` takes.
        :raises requests.exceptions.JSONDecodeError: If the response body does not
            contain valid json.

        if not self.encoding and self.content and len(self.content) > 3:
            # No encoding set. JSON RFC 4627 section 3 states we should expect
            # UTF-8, -16 or -32. Detect which one to use; If the detection or
            # decoding fails, fall back to `self.text` (using charset_normalizer to make
            # a best guess).

                    # but some other 8-bit codec.  This is an RFC violation,
                    # and the server didn't bother to tell us what codec *was*

                    pass

self = <Response [520]>, kwargs = {}

    def json(self, **kwargs):
        r"""Decodes the JSON response body (if any) as a Python object.
    
        This may return a dictionary, list, etc. depending on what is in the response.
    
        :param \*\*kwargs: Optional arguments that ``json.loads`` takes.
        :raises requests.exceptions.JSONDecodeError: If the response body does not
            contain valid json.
        """
    
        if not self.encoding and self.content and len(self.content) > 3:
            # No encoding set. JSON RFC 4627 section 3 states we should expect
            # UTF-8, -16 or -32. Detect which one to use; If the detection or
            # decoding fails, fall back to `self.text` (using charset_normalizer to make
            # a best guess).
            encoding = guess_json_utf(self.content)
            if encoding is not None:
                try:
                    return complexjson.loads(self.content.decode(encoding), **kwargs)
                except UnicodeDecodeError:
                    # Wrong UTF codec detected; usually because it's not UTF-8
                    # but some other 8-bit codec.  This is an RFC violation,
                    # and the server didn't bother to tell us what codec *was*
                    # used.
                    pass
                except JSONDecodeError as e:
                    raise RequestsJSONDecodeError(e.msg, e.doc, e.pos)

        except JSONDecodeError as e:
            # Catch JSON-related errors and raise as requests.JSONDecodeError

self = <PreparedRequest [POST]>, url = '/api/auth/login', params = OrderedDict()

    def prepare_url(self, url, params):
        """Prepares the given HTTP URL."""
        #: Accept objects that have string representations.
        #: We're unable to blindly call unicode/str functions
        #: as this will include the bytestring indicator (b'')
        #: on python 3.x.
        #: https://github.com/psf/requests/pull/2238
        if isinstance(url, bytes):
            url = url.decode("utf8")
        else:
            url = str(url)
    
        # Remove leading whitespaces from url
        url = url.lstrip()
    
        # Don't do any URL preparation for non-HTTP schemes like `mailto`,
        # `data` etc to work around exceptions from `url_parse`, which
        # handles RFC 3986 only.
        if ":" in url and not url.lower().startswith("http"):
            self.url = url
            return
    
        # Support for unicode domain names and paths.
        try:
            scheme, auth, host, port, path, query, fragment = parse_url(url)
        except LocationParseError as e:
            raise InvalidURL(*e.args)
    
        if not scheme:
>           raise MissingSchema(
                f"Invalid URL {url!r}: No scheme supplied. "
                f"Perhaps you meant https://{url}?"
            )
E           requests.exceptions.MissingSchema: Invalid URL '/api/auth/login': No scheme supplied. Perhaps you meant https:///api/auth/login?

self = <PreparedRequest [POST]>, url = '/api/auth/login', params = OrderedDict()

    def prepare_url(self, url, params):
        """Prepares the given HTTP URL."""
        #: Accept objects that have string representations.
        #: We're unable to blindly call unicode/str functions
        #: as this will include the bytestring indicator (b'')
        #: on python 3.x.
        #: https://github.com/psf/requests/pull/2238
        if isinstance(url, bytes):
            url = url.decode("utf8")
        else:
            url = str(url)
    
        # Remove leading whitespaces from url
        url = url.lstrip()
    
        # Don't do any URL preparation for non-HTTP schemes like `mailto`,
        # `data` etc to work around exceptions from `url_parse`, which
        # handles RFC 3986 only.
        if ":" in url and not url.lower().startswith("http"):
            self.url = url
            return
    
        # Support for unicode domain names and paths.
        try:
            scheme, auth, host, port, path, query, fragment = parse_url(url)
        except LocationParseError as e:
            raise InvalidURL(*e.args)
    
        if not scheme:
>           raise MissingSchema(
                f"Invalid URL {url!r}: No scheme supplied. "
                f"Perhaps you meant https://{url}?"
            )
E           requests.exceptions.MissingSchema: Invalid URL '/api/auth/login': No scheme supplied. Perhaps you meant https:///api/auth/login?

self = <PreparedRequest [POST]>, url = '/api/auth/login', params = OrderedDict()

    def prepare_url(self, url, params):
        """Prepares the given HTTP URL."""
        #: Accept objects that have string representations.
        #: We're unable to blindly call unicode/str functions
        #: as this will include the bytestring indicator (b'')
        #: on python 3.x.
        #: https://github.com/psf/requests/pull/2238
        if isinstance(url, bytes):
            url = url.decode("utf8")
        else:
            url = str(url)
    
        # Remove leading whitespaces from url
        url = url.lstrip()
    
        # Don't do any URL preparation for non-HTTP schemes like `mailto`,
        # `data` etc to work around exceptions from `url_parse`, which
        # handles RFC 3986 only.
        if ":" in url and not url.lower().startswith("http"):
            self.url = url
            return
    
        # Support for unicode domain names and paths.
        try:
            scheme, auth, host, port, path, query, fragment = parse_url(url)
        except LocationParseError as e:
            raise InvalidURL(*e.args)
    
        if not scheme:
>           raise MissingSchema(
                f"Invalid URL {url!r}: No scheme supplied. "
                f"Perhaps you meant https://{url}?"
            )
E           requests.exceptions.MissingSchema: Invalid URL '/api/auth/login': No scheme supplied. Perhaps you meant https:///api/auth/login?

self = <PreparedRequest [POST]>, url = '/api/auth/login', params = OrderedDict()

    def prepare_url(self, url, params):
        """Prepares the given HTTP URL."""
        #: Accept objects that have string representations.
        #: We're unable to blindly call unicode/str functions
        #: as this will include the bytestring indicator (b'')
        #: on python 3.x.
        #: https://github.com/psf/requests/pull/2238
        if isinstance(url, bytes):
            url = url.decode("utf8")
        else:
            url = str(url)
    
        # Remove leading whitespaces from url
        url = url.lstrip()
    
        # Don't do any URL preparation for non-HTTP schemes like `mailto`,
        # `data` etc to work around exceptions from `url_parse`, which
        # handles RFC 3986 only.
        if ":" in url and not url.lower().startswith("http"):
            self.url = url
            return
    
        # Support for unicode domain names and paths.
        try:
            scheme, auth, host, port, path, query, fragment = parse_url(url)
        except LocationParseError as e:
            raise InvalidURL(*e.args)
    
        if not scheme:
>           raise MissingSchema(
                f"Invalid URL {url!r}: No scheme supplied. "
                f"Perhaps you meant https://{url}?"

self = <PreparedRequest [POST]>, url = '/api/auth/login', params = OrderedDict()

    def prepare_url(self, url, params):
        """Prepares the given HTTP URL."""
        #: Accept objects that have string representations.
        #: We're unable to blindly call unicode/str functions
        #: as this will include the bytestring indicator (b'')
        #: on python 3.x.
        #: https://github.com/psf/requests/pull/2238
        if isinstance(url, bytes):
            url = url.decode("utf8")
        else:
            url = str(url)
    
        # Remove leading whitespaces from url
        url = url.lstrip()
    
        # Don't do any URL preparation for non-HTTP schemes like `mailto`,
        # `data` etc to work around exceptions from `url_parse`, which
        # handles RFC 3986 only.
        if ":" in url and not url.lower().startswith("http"):
            self.url = url
            return
    
        # Support for unicode domain names and paths.
        try:
            scheme, auth, host, port, path, query, fragment = parse_url(url)
        except LocationParseError as e:
            raise InvalidURL(*e.args)
    
        if not scheme:
>           raise MissingSchema(
                f"Invalid URL {url!r}: No scheme supplied. "
                f"Perhaps you meant https://{url}?"
            )
E           requests.exceptions.MissingSchema: Invalid URL '/api/auth/login': No scheme supplied. Perhaps you meant https:///api/auth/login?

self = <PreparedRequest [POST]>, url = '/api/auth/login', params = OrderedDict()

    def prepare_url(self, url, params):
        """Prepares the given HTTP URL."""
        #: Accept objects that have string representations.
        #: We're unable to blindly call unicode/str functions
        #: as this will include the bytestring indicator (b'')
        #: on python 3.x.
        #: https://github.com/psf/requests/pull/2238
        if isinstance(url, bytes):
            url = url.decode("utf8")
        else:
            url = str(url)
    
        # Remove leading whitespaces from url
        url = url.lstrip()
    
        # Don't do any URL preparation for non-HTTP schemes like `mailto`,
        # `data` etc to work around exceptions from `url_parse`, which
        # handles RFC 3986 only.
        if ":" in url and not url.lower().startswith("http"):
            self.url = url
            return
    
        # Support for unicode domain names and paths.
        try:
            scheme, auth, host, port, path, query, fragment = parse_url(url)
        except LocationParseError as e:
            raise InvalidURL(*e.args)
    
        if not scheme:

self = <PreparedRequest [POST]>, url = '/api/auth/login', params = OrderedDict()

    def prepare_url(self, url, params):
        """Prepares the given HTTP URL."""
        #: Accept objects that have string representations.
        #: We're unable to blindly call unicode/str functions
        #: as this will include the bytestring indicator (b'')
        #: on python 3.x.
        #: https://github.com/psf/requests/pull/2238
        if isinstance(url, bytes):
            url = url.decode("utf8")
        else:
            url = str(url)
    
        # Remove leading whitespaces from url
        url = url.lstrip()
    
        # Don't do any URL preparation for non-HTTP schemes like `mailto`,
        # `data` etc to work around exceptions from `url_parse`, which
        # handles RFC 3986 only.
        if ":" in url and not url.lower().startswith("http"):
            self.url = url
            return
    
        # Support for unicode domain names and paths.
        try:
            scheme, auth, host, port, path, query, fragment = parse_url(url)
        except LocationParseError as e:
            raise InvalidURL(*e.args)
    
        if not scheme:
>           raise MissingSchema(
                f"Invalid URL {url!r}: No scheme supplied. "
                f"Perhaps you meant https://{url}?"
            )

self = <PreparedRequest [POST]>, url = '/api/auth/login', params = OrderedDict()

    def prepare_url(self, url, params):
        """Prepares the given HTTP URL."""
        #: Accept objects that have string representations.
        #: We're unable to blindly call unicode/str functions
        #: as this will include the bytestring indicator (b'')
        #: on python 3.x.
        #: https://github.com/psf/requests/pull/2238
        if isinstance(url, bytes):
            url = url.decode("utf8")
        else:
            url = str(url)
    
        # Remove leading whitespaces from url
        url = url.lstrip()
    
        # Don't do any URL preparation for non-HTTP schemes like `mailto`,
        # `data` etc to work around exceptions from `url_parse`, which
        # handles RFC 3986 only.
        if ":" in url and not url.lower().startswith("http"):
            self.url = url
            return
    
        # Support for unicode domain names and paths.
        try:
            scheme, auth, host, port, path, query, fragment = parse_url(url)
        except LocationParseError as e:
            raise InvalidURL(*e.args)
    
        if not scheme:
>           raise MissingSchema(
                f"Invalid URL {url!r}: No scheme supplied. "
                f"Perhaps you meant https://{url}?"
            )

self = <PreparedRequest [POST]>, url = '/api/auth/login', params = OrderedDict()

    def prepare_url(self, url, params):
        """Prepares the given HTTP URL."""
        #: Accept objects that have string representations.
        #: We're unable to blindly call unicode/str functions
        #: as this will include the bytestring indicator (b'')
        #: on python 3.x.
        #: https://github.com/psf/requests/pull/2238
        if isinstance(url, bytes):
            url = url.decode("utf8")
        else:
            url = str(url)
    
        # Remove leading whitespaces from url
        url = url.lstrip()
    
        # Don't do any URL preparation for non-HTTP schemes like `mailto`,
        # `data` etc to work around exceptions from `url_parse`, which
        # handles RFC 3986 only.
        if ":" in url and not url.lower().startswith("http"):
            self.url = url
            return
    
        # Support for unicode domain names and paths.
        try:
            scheme, auth, host, port, path, query, fragment = parse_url(url)
        except LocationParseError as e:
            raise InvalidURL(*e.args)
    
        if not scheme:
>           raise MissingSchema(
                f"Invalid URL {url!r}: No scheme supplied. "
                f"Perhaps you meant https://{url}?"
            )
E           requests.exceptions.MissingSchema: Invalid URL '/api/auth/login': No scheme supplied. Perhaps you meant https:///api/auth/login?

self = <PreparedRequest [POST]>, url = '/api/auth/login', params = OrderedDict()

    def prepare_url(self, url, params):
        """Prepares the given HTTP URL."""
        #: Accept objects that have string representations.
        #: We're unable to blindly call unicode/str functions
        #: as this will include the bytestring indicator (b'')
        #: on python 3.x.
        #: https://github.com/psf/requests/pull/2238
        if isinstance(url, bytes):
            url = url.decode("utf8")
        else:
            url = str(url)
    
        # Remove leading whitespaces from url
        url = url.lstrip()
    
        # Don't do any URL preparation for non-HTTP schemes like `mailto`,
        # `data` etc to work around exceptions from `url_parse`, which
        # handles RFC 3986 only.
        if ":" in url and not url.lower().startswith("http"):
            self.url = url
            return
    
        # Support for unicode domain names and paths.
        try:
            scheme, auth, host, port, path, query, fragment = parse_url(url)
        except LocationParseError as e:
            raise InvalidURL(*e.args)
    
        if not scheme:
>           raise MissingSchema(
                f"Invalid URL {url!r}: No scheme supplied. "
                f"Perhaps you meant https://{url}?"
            )
